**EXP-1391 -- shadow<->streamd lock collisions, measured from the kernel.** The one new signal in the journal was three `flush FAILED (... Conflicting lock is held in ... PID 2463722)` lines in 20 min; PID 2463722 is `hyxlab-shadow.service`'s 20s read poll on `hyxstream.duckdb`. The ledger (`data/stream_stalls.jsonl`) attributes to the shadow, by day: 10-17 13, 10-18 23, 10-19 15, 10-20 20, 10-21 10, 10-22 15, 10-23 23, **10-24 43, 10-25 49, 10-26 50 by 14:15Z (3.5/h)** -- every one a single failed flush (`fails 1`, `hold_lower_s 0.0`, span 15.9-16.3s = the ledger's floor), rows held and written on the next tick, `spilled 0`, peak_pending 56-136k. Lossless, but a 2-4x step at the 10-23 coverage expansion (#134) is drift to explain. **The shadow's own reading, hourly from its 300s stats line:** `held_s_total` / polls = 0.124-0.129 s/poll on 10-25 01-03Z (1.5-2.5k events/poll) rising to 0.150-0.172 s/poll on 10-26 (4.3-6.6k events/poll) -- the per-poll hold tracks events per poll, the 10-25T10 hour's 3.514 s/poll is the 10-25 QA's 872s hold slowing the shadow's reads (#141, fixed). The shadow's contended polls (+8-12/h) match streamd's hold from the other side: ~220 flushes/h x ~0.8s = 4.9% of the hour x 180 polls = ~9/h. **Measured from OUTSIDE both processes** (no attach, no interference: sampled `/proc/locks` for the archive's inode every 2.1 ms for 180s, 84,750 samples, 14:18-14:21Z):

```
('2394988', 'WRITE') holds 11 total 8.89s frac 0.0494 p50 0.812 max 0.940   # streamd flush: 0.81s each, one per 16.4s (15s sleep + the flush)
('2463722', 'READ')  holds  9 total 1.50s frac 0.0083 p50 0.167 max 0.188   # shadow poll: 0.167s each, one per 20s
```

The shadow's self-reported 0.159 s/poll (14:xxZ stats) is within 5% of the kernel's 0.167 -- `held_stream_conn` starts its clock after `stream_conn` returns (open 22ms + two SETs + `spill_cap`), and that is the whole gap; the instrument is honest, which is the first time anything outside the holder has said so (#91/#103: "only the holder measures a hold" -- the kernel does too, for an fcntl lock, at 2 ms resolution). **Independence model:** collisions/h = flushes/h x shadow hold fraction = 220 x 0.0083 = **1.8/h**; observed 10-25 2.0/h (49/24h) and **10-26 3.5/h** -- the 10-25 day fits, today runs 1.9x over with a hold fraction only 12% higher. OPEN, not resolved this pass: the two loops are fixed-period (16.4s and 20.17s), so the relative phase advances 3.77s per shadow cycle rather than mixing, and a contended shadow poll re-phases the shadow to just after a flush ends -- the count is a property of the beat, not of the fractions, and a day's count can sit well off the product. Testable from the ledger's `started` timestamps modulo the beat; not done. **Not fixing streamd** (a sub-second retry on refusal would turn each 15s deferral into ~0.2s but rewrites the ledger's `fails`/`period_s` semantics, #92/#103) and **not fixing the shadow** (a reflink snapshot per poll holds the live file ~0.2s itself, #141): 85 lossless 15s deferrals a day at 0.8% of the writer's time is a number to watch, not a defect. Watch line: the per-poll hold at 0.3 s (= 2x today, ~4/h by the model) is where this gets a pass of its own. **14:15Z RUN, NEXT:** kalshi done line (~15:20Z: wall vs 12.5h, flush legs, 429s); arm ~1500s if the deadline allows. **THE PREMISE OF NEXT-PASS ITEM (1) WAS FALSE, AND CHECKING IT WAS THE FIRST THING THIS PASS DID.** The queue carried "shorten QA's stream hold -- 13m39s daily". That 819.0s came from #102's AD-HOC run at 02:29Z, the one whose own write-up records that it made a 1,804-test suite take 15.5 minutes. **The first TIMER measurement, 09-29 10:00Z, is 47.1s** -- `hyxlab.duckdb 1.3s; hyxshadow.duckdb 0.0s; hyxstream.duckdb 47.1s`, in a unit whose entire run was 49s wall (05:00:00 -> 05:00:49 CDT). So the exclusion QA buys is ~17x load-bound, not query-bound, and the scoping shipped in 94e32ca was already in place for BOTH numbers. #102's own closing sentence -- "before running any standing report ad hoc against the live archives, know that you are buying that exclusion now rather than at its timer" -- turned out to be the whole explanation of its own headline figure, and nobody applied it to the figure. **Item (1) is retired as stated**: 47.1s of daily exclusion against a 15s flush cadence is inside the buffer design (the episode it caused peaked at 6,965 rows and spilled 0), and there is nothing to move outside the hold -- the 38s inside it is the poly delta-replay query reading 421,510 levels out of that file, which cannot be computed anywhere else. Re-open it only if a timer run, not an ad-hoc one, measures a hold that spills.

**AND THE TWO ARMS NOW AGREE, TWO-SIDED, FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THIS ARCHIVE.** The same QA run is the holder of a stall episode in `data/stream_stalls.jsonl`: started 10:00:08.2Z, closed 10:00:57.1Z, span 48.9s, 3 failed flushes, `sample_gap_max_s` 15.0, `hold_lower_s` 30.0, holder named at the collision as `hyxlab-qa.service pid 3864557` -- the same pid systemd shows for the run. **30.0s <= 47.1s <= 48.9s**: the victim's measured lower bound and its span bracket the holder's own clock from both sides. That is the post-fix reading NEXT-PASS item (2) asked for, and it is the first time the #92/#103 instruments have been checked against a holder that was independently measuring itself. Three more post-fix episodes the same morning behave as designed: two single-failure stalls (09:52Z, 11:35Z) publish `hold_lower_s` 0.0 and `sample_gap_max_s` 0.0 -- a lone failure proves no hold at all, exactly as #92's correction requires, where the old `(fails-1)*15.0` would also have said 0.0 but for the wrong reason.

**THE PASS'S SHIP: #103 SURVIVED ONE LEVEL DOWN.** EXP-1390's rule is that only a flush which OPENED the file may end a stall episode. The flusher was taught it; `Daemon._drain_locked` was not. It called the count-only `flush()` and reported `self.stalls.ok(now)` unconditionally -- and that is the worse of the two places, because the drain runs while the daemon is dying, so its record is the LAST thing the ledger ever says about that episode and a `closed` written there is what every later reader differences forever. Fixed to `flush_outcome()` gated on `touched`; an untouched drain is neither a recovery nor a loss, so the episode is handed to `interrupted` (a no-op when none is open), which says the stall outlived the process.

**LATENT, AND BY AN INVARIANT NOTHING ENFORCED -- SO THE INVARIANT IS NOW A TEST.** An episode opens only from a flush that RAISED, which cannot happen past `flush_outcome`'s early return, so there were rows or a sidecar and the drain's own flush therefore opens the file. Asserted at the real store rather than written in prose, because `drain_rows_estimate` reads an unstattable sidecar as zero (documented, deliberate): the arithmetic that keeps this line unreachable is one suppressed `OSError` wide. Found the usual accomplice while wiring it -- **three separate `_Store` test doubles** (`test_stream_stalls`, `test_drain_budget`, `test_flush_concurrency`) modelled `flush()` as always-succeeds-always-touches, so all three were green against the defect by construction; all three now mirror the real early return.

**Suite 1812 -> 1815**, lint clean; the mutant (collapse the branch back to a bare `stalls.ok(now)`) reddens only its own claim, verified by diffing the mutated source before reading the colour. Promoted cb22360: `hyxlab-stream` restarted onto it at 09:27:02 CDT and its shutdown drain printed its lines (the #54 witness again); `hyxlab-shadow` correctly deferred by the young-run guard.

**NEW WATCH ITEM -- AN OUTSIDE PROJECT HOLDS THIS DAEMON'S ARCHIVE.** 12 of the 373 stall records name `hylshi-archive-reconcile.service` as the holder of `data/hyxstream.duckdb`, three of them this morning (09:50-09:52Z, pids 3860949/3861074/3861645, one 49.2s episode peaking at 6,409 buffered rows). That is a SIBLING project's unit taking the lock on a file this repo's ops rules assign to `collector.streamd` alone. It is not an alarm -- the holds are short, the buffer absorbed them, and a read-only attach excluding a writer is exactly what EXP-1390 measured -- but every lock discipline in `.claude/rules/ops.md` is written as if this repo's units were the only openers, and they are not. Worth costing before the next reader is added on either side.

NEXT PASS: (1) **the sweep's line in the new per-file shape** -- `held 7550 on hyxlab.duckdb for <N>s` against the calibrated 2959.0s, paired with the collector's `wait_s`; carried a ninth time, still mid-run at pass start. (2) shadow's first `shadow_stream_holds` row at its next NATURAL restart (panel now 5/10; do not force it). (3) the rest of item (6): `simui`, `backup`, `bookreplay` and `streamstore` still record attach rows nothing publishes. (4) the #98/#93 class sweep continues -- `_check_freshness`'s 4-cadence tolerance, `sweep_log` freshness, the tape-coverage grace windows, the poly `gap` rows -- and #103's question with it: which other detectors read an operation's success as evidence about a resource it never reached? (5) cost the hylshi cross-project holder above. (6) the `UNCAPPED` debt list, 7 units. (7) the PONG fix still needs a window containing an idle burst; carried a twenty-first time. (8) the favorite-longshot pre-registration over the atlas quoted tiers -- **CHECK FIRST: the fav-long FAMILY was closed 08-23**, so it must be justified as a NEW subject or not written at all. (9) weather 09-30; econ late Sept. (10) #90's and #89's sweeps both still open.

## Where the project is

**The data-collection layer is structurally complete** (user direction
2026-07-07: all data first, then the simulation platform). Everything
either venue still serves is captured or capturing on timers, and rot
trips alarms:

- **Kalshi**: 2.6M hourly candles (60d capture, 35.7k markets, 8-category
  allowlist — sports/entertainment/politics exclusion USER-CONFIRMED
  2026-07-08); trade tape 5.6M+ prints (retro-pass finishing overnight;
  forward capture rides the daily sweep); live WS books (watchlist
  series) + exchange-wide trade firehose.
- **Polymarket**: metadata + volume/liquidity series + ~60d hourly price
  history for all markets ≥$10k volume (universe now ~4,600 and growing)
  + trade tails (API caps at last 3,000/market — forward tape is our
  WS); live books for top-50 volume markets' tokens. **2026-07-08:
  Gamma capped /markets offset at 2000 hours after that day's sweep —
  enumeration moved to /markets/keyset same day (fix promoted to
  stable before the next 05:00 run; see [venues](venues.md)).
- **Ground truth**: 33k MOS forecasts, climate observations.
- **Timers**: collect 5min; poly sweep 05:00; kalshi sweep 06:10;
  QA 07:00 UTC (both archives; tape-coverage + freshness alarms).
- **Deployment**: daemons run from the `stable` worktree;
  `scripts/promote.sh` is the only shipping path. Import boundary
  (collection ↛ sim) test-enforced.

**Sim machinery already standing**: sim v2 (order lifecycle, accounting
invariants), four correctness gates, capability guard, latency model
(`Simulator(latency=Δ)`), BookReplayer (stream → ms snapshots; first
Tier-2 sweep: 1s latency ≈ +0.4¢/contract), simui replay terminal with
a proven chunked≡one-shot replay equivalence (see
[simulation-honesty](simulation-honesty.md)). Suite green (count moves; test-gate enforces).

**Falsification record**: weather v1 pre-reg FAIL (−$425, fees decide).

iteration every 6h (02/08/14/20:15 UTC) against the investigation
ladder — the never-stop directive as infrastructure, surviving
interactive sessions. flock-guarded; hooks + hard rules bind headless
runs identically. Knobs: cadence (timer), turn cap (autoloop.sh),
permission mode.

## Watch items (not yet alarming)

- **External box-wide memory pressure (2026-07-19→)**: sibling
  workspace `hytest` (`impl.m1.gate`, 16 shards ≈ 58G of 60G, no swap)
  starves everything; kernel OOM killed stream ×2, one autoloop
  replay, the 07-20 poly sweep and QA. Hardened 07-20: oneshot units
  carry `OOMScoreAdjust=500` so batch work dies before capture
  daemons. The user's job — do not kill it. While it runs: skip
  memory-heavy ad-hoc work (divergence replays, atlas over full
  archive is ~fine but monitor — MEASURED 2026-09-26 and no longer a
  guess on the LOCK side: atlas holds the archive 0.96–1.07s over 408
  buckets, four runs, so its exclusion of the 5-minute collector is one
  second; memory is still the open half of that caveat), expect
  degraded WS connects, and
  verify the next poly sweep (05:00) and QA (07:00) complete. If a
  matured poly day-bucket lands short, the ~2-day maturation backfill
  covers a single missed sweep.

- **DuckDB vs cgroup memory caps**: hyxlab-shadow was kernel-OOM-killed
  at boot twice (2026-07-11, 2026-07-12 — systemd auto-restart
  recovered both) because DuckDB's default memory_limit scales with
  SYSTEM RAM, far above the unit's MemoryMax=1G; the seed-time ORDER BY
  blew the cap. FIXED for shadow 2026-07-12 (`stream_conn`: 512MiB
  engine cap, 2 threads, spill to `data/duckspill-shadow`, and it now
  uses the mandated `connect_retry`). **A SECOND, distinct OOM class
  hit 2026-07-18 22:03 UTC**: mid-run kill after 2.3 days from the
  per-snapshot in-memory equity curve (~340MB/day) — FIXED 2026-07-19
  (5d7e4aa: running max_drawdown + shadow trims the curve per poll).
  VERIFIED CLOSED 2026-07-20: run 20260719T082112 plateaued —
  VmRSS 305MB@6h → 285MB@12h → 270MB@24h (HWM 835MB, the DuckDB seed
  spike) — and survived the 07-19/07-20 box-wide OOM storm untouched;
  no third accumulator. `hyxlab-simui` shares the 1G cap and replays big
  archive windows — same exposure (it holds full curves by design for
  bounded windows); apply bounds if it ever OOMs. **A THIRD class
  found 2026-08-07 02:15 before it fired**: the hourly metadata
  refresh loads the FULL markets table, which the 08-02 breadth
  widening grew to 486k rows / ~430MB (+13k rows/day) — swap-reload
  double-holds it and the daemon reached HWM 966MB of 1G. FIXED
  b962b5c (filtered load + held-market pinning, ~60MB); running
  process bridged with a --runtime MemoryMax=2G until its next
  restart. Pattern across all three: any per-poll or per-reload state
  proportional to the ARCHIVE (equity curve ∝ snapshots, metadata ∝
  markets table) eventually outgrows a fixed cgroup cap — bound state
  by what the daemon can ACT on, not by what the archive holds.

- **Poly swept universe decline is partly a measurement artifact**
  (found 2026-07-12): day-buckets MATURE for ~2 days as later sweeps
  backfill price history into past days (Jul 10 read 5,692 on Jul 11
  but 6,672 on Jul 12). The tripwire compares a fresh (immature)
  yesterday against matured peaks — biased toward false alarms; the
  0.5 threshold absorbs the ~15–20% maturation effect. True trend is
  a mild decline (7.2–7.4k steady-state), not the ~5%/day slide
  first estimated. Watch only if matured days trend below ~5k or the
  sweep runtime keeps growing.

## Hard rules in force

Zero capital without pre-registered Tier-2+ PASS **and** explicit user
authorization. No retro-rescues of failed strategies. Probe before
build. Every new store writer ships with a stored-timestamp assertion
(mistakes #10). Vacuous backtests must refuse to run.)
